<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt2" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Redirect","href":"./Template:Redirect"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Holy Language"},"2":{"wt":"the language so called by John Dee"},"3":{"wt":"Enochian"},"4":{"wt":"the concept of a divine language"},"5":{"wt":"Divine language"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1368532237" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt3" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA">"Holy Language" redirects here. For the language so called by John Dee, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enochian" title="Enochian">Enochian</a>. For the concept of a divine language, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_language" title="Divine language">Divine language</a>.</div>

data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Navbar/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output class="nv-edit"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Anthropology_of_religion" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Anthropology of religion"><abbr <p id="mwDw">A <b id="mwEA">sacred language</b>, <b id="mwEQ">liturgical language, lingua sacra,</b> or <b id="mwEg">holy language</b> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language" title="Language" id="mwEw">language</a> that is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literary_language" title="Literary language" id="mwFA">cultivated</a> and used primarily for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion" title="Religion" id="mwFQ">religious</a> reasons (like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_service" title="Church service" id="mwFg">church service</a>) by people who speak another, primary language in their daily lives.</p>

<p id="mwFw">Some religions, or parts of them, regard the language of their <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_texts" title="Sacred texts" class="mw-redirect" id="mwGA">sacred texts</a> as in itself sacred. These include <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Latin" title="Ecclesiastical Latin" id="mwGQ">Ecclesiastical Latin</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church" id="mwGg">Roman Catholicism</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language" id="mwGw">Hebrew</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism" title="Judaism" id="mwHA">Judaism</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic" title="Arabic" id="mwHQ">Arabic</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam" title="Islam" id="mwHg">Islam</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avestan" title="Avestan" id="mwHw">Avestan</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism" title="Zoroastrianism" id="mwIA">Zoroastrianism</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwIQ">Sanskrit</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language" id="mwIg">Tamil</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism" id="mwIw">Hinduism</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meitei_language" title="Meitei language" id="mwJA">Meitei</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanamahism" title="Sanamahism" id="mwJQ">Sanamahism</a>. By contrast <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism" id="mwJg">Buddhism</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity" id="mwJw">Christian denominations</a> outside of Catholicism do not generally regard their sacred languages as sacred in themselves.</p>

<p id="mwKw">A sacred language is often the language which was spoken and written in the society in which a religion's sacred texts were first set down; these texts thereafter become fixed and holy, remaining frozen and immune to later <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linguistics" title="Linguistics" id="mwLA">linguistic</a> developments.<sup about="#mwt13" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwLQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt16" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwMQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt19" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-taylorfrancis.com_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"taylorfrancis.com"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-taylorfrancis.com-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-taylorfrancis.com-4" id="mwOQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOw">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPA">]</span></span></a></sup> (An exception to this is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucumí_language" title="Lucumí language" id="mwPQ">Lucumí</a>, a ritual lexicon of the Cuban strain of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santería" title="Santería" id="mwPg">Santería</a> religion, with no standardized <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logical_form_(linguistics)" title="Logical form (linguistics)" id="mwPw">form</a>.)</p>

<p id="mwQA">Once a language becomes associated with religious worship, its believers may ascribe virtues to the language of worship that they would not give to their native tongues.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt24" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwQQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"April 2011"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_April_2011" about="#mwt24"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt24" id="mwQg"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> In the case of sacred texts, there is a fear of losing authenticity and accuracy by a translation or re-translation, and difficulties in achieving acceptance for a new version of a text. A sacred language is typically vested with a solemnity and dignity that the vernacular lacks. Consequently, the training of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clergy" title="Clergy" id="mwQw">clergy</a> in the use of a sacred language becomes an important cultural investment, and their use of the tongue is perceived to give them access to a body of knowledge that untrained <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laity" title="Laity" id="mwRA">laypeople</a> cannot (or should not) access.</p>

<p id="mwRQ">Because sacred languages are ascribed with virtues that the vernacular is not seen to have,<sup about="#mwt25" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-taylorfrancis.com_4-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"taylorfrancis.com"}}'><a href="#cite_note-taylorfrancis.com-4" id="mwRg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwRw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">]</span></span></a></sup> these typically preserve characteristics lost in the course of language development. In some cases, the sacred language is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extinct_language" title="Extinct language" id="mwSg">dead language</a>, while in others, it may simply reflect <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaism" title="Archaism" id="mwSw">archaic</a> forms of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Living_language" title="Living language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTA">living language</a>. For instance, 17th-century elements of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language" title="English language" id="mwTQ">English language</a> remain current in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism" id="mwTg">Protestant Christian</a> worship through the use of the <i id="mwTw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_James_Version" title="King James Version" id="mwUA">King James Bible</a></i> from 1611, or older versions of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican" title="Anglican" class="mw-redirect" id="mwUQ">Anglican</a> <i id="mwUg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Common_Prayer" title="Book of Common Prayer" id="mwUw">Book of Common Prayer</a></i>. In more extreme cases, the language has changed so much from the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_text" title="Sacred text" class="mw-redirect" id="mwVA">sacred texts</a> that the liturgy is barely comprehensible without special training. For example, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_liturgical_rites" title="Latin liturgical rites" id="mwVQ">liturgy</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church" title="Latin Church" id="mwVg">Roman Catholic Church</a> remained in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin" title="Latin" id="mwVw">Latin</a> after the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Council_of_Tours" title="Third Council of Tours" class="mw-redirect" id="mwWA">Council of Tours in 813</a> ordered preaching in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_languages" title="Romance languages" id="mwWQ">local Romance</a> or German, because Latin was no longer understood. Similarly, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Church_Slavonic" title="Old Church Slavonic" id="mwWg">Old Church Slavonic</a> is incomprehensible to speakers of modern <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_languages" title="Slavic languages" id="mwWw">Slavic languages</a>, unless they study it.</p>

<p id="mwXA">Sacred languages are distinct from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_language" title="Divine language" id="mwXQ">divine languages</a>, which are languages ascribed to the divine (i.e. God or gods) and may not necessarily be natural languages.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt26" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwXg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"August 2013"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_August_2013" about="#mwt26"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt26" id="mwXw"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> The concept, as expressed by the name of a script, for example in <i id="mwYA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devanagari" title="Devanagari" id="mwYQ">Dēvanāgarī</a></i>, is the name of a script that roughly means "[script] of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prayagraj" title="Prayagraj" id="mwYg">city of gods</a>", and is used to write many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_India" title="Languages of India" id="mwYw">Indian languages</a>.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt27" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwZA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cn","href":"./Template:Cn"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"February 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_February_2026" about="#mwt27"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt27" id="mwZQ"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_pages_needing_factual_verification" about="#mwt28" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwZg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"vn","href":"./Template:Vn"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"February 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_factual_verification_from_February_2026" about="#mwt28"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template " style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt28" id="mwZw"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (February 2026)">verification needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwaA" aria-labelledby="Buddhism"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Buddhism">Buddhism</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Buddhism"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwaQ">When the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautama_Buddha" title="Gautama Buddha" class="mw-redirect" id="mwag">Buddha</a>'s <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutra" title="Sutra" id="mwaw">sutras</a> were first written down, probably in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali" title="Pali" id="mwbA">Pali</a>, there were around 20 schools, each with their own version derived from the original. The present <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pāli_Canon" title="Pāli Canon" class="mw-redirect" id="mwbQ">Pāli Canon</a> originates from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamrashatiya" title="Tamrashatiya" id="mwbg">Tamrashatiya school</a>. The Chinese and Tibetan canons mainly derive from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarvastivada" title="Sarvastivada" id="mwbw">Sarvastivada</a>, originally written in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwcA">Sanskrit</a>, of which fragments remain. The texts were translated into <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language" id="mwcQ">Chinese</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lhasa_Tibetan" title="Lhasa Tibetan" id="mwcg">Tibetan</a>.<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwdw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada" title="Theravada" id="mweA">Theravada</a> Buddhism uses Pali as its main liturgical language and prefers that scripture be studied in the original Pali.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt32" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mweQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"June 2019"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_June_2019" about="#mwt32"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt32" id="mweg"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> Pali is derived from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwew">Sanskrit</a>.<sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Norman_6-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Norman"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Norman-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Norman-6" id="mwfA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfw">]</span></span></a></sup> In Thailand Pali is transliterated into the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_alphabet" title="Thai alphabet" class="mw-redirect" id="mwgA">Thai alphabet</a>,<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt33" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwgQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"June 2019"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_June_2019" about="#mwt33"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt33" id="mwgg"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> resulting in a Thai pronunciation of the Pali language. Something similar also happened in Myanmar, where Pali is also transliterated into the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burmese_alphabet" title="Burmese alphabet" id="mwgw">Burmese alphabet</a>, resulting in a Burmese pronunciation of Pali.</p>

<p id="mwhA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana" title="Mahayana" id="mwhQ">Mahayana</a> Buddhism, now only followed by a small minority in South Asia, makes little use of its original language, Sanskrit, mostly using versions of the local language. In East Asia, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese" title="Classical Chinese" id="mwhg">Classical Chinese</a> is mainly used.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt37" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwhw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"June 2019"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_June_2019" about="#mwt37"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt37" id="mwiA"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> In Japan, texts are written in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanji" title="Kanji" id="mwiQ">Chinese characters</a> and read out or recited with the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanji#Readings" title="Kanji" id="mwig">Japanese pronunciations</a> of their constituent characters.<sup about="#mwt40" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwiw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwjw">In <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vajrayana" title="Vajrayana" id="mwkA">Vajrayana</a> Buddhism, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism" id="mwkQ">Tibetan Buddhism</a> is the main surviving school, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Tibetan" title="Classical Tibetan" id="mwkg">Classical Tibetan</a> is the main language used for study,<sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwkw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlg">]</span></span></a></sup> although the Tibetan Buddhist canon was also translated into other languages, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolian_language" title="Mongolian language" id="mwlw">Mongolian</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchu_language" title="Manchu language" id="mwmA">Manchu</a>.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwmQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnA">]</span></span></a></sup> Many items of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_Buddhist_literature" title="Sanskrit Buddhist literature" id="mwnQ">Sanskrit Buddhist literature</a> have been preserved because they were exported to Tibet, with copies of unknown ancient Sanskrit texts surfacing in Tibet as recently as 2003.<sup about="#mwt47" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwng"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoA">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Sanskrit was valued in Tibet as the elegant language of the gods.<sup about="#mwt50" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwog"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwow"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpA">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Although in Tibetan Buddhist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deity_yoga" title="Deity yoga" id="mwpg">deity yoga</a> the rest of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadhana" title="Sadhana" class="mw-redirect" id="mwpw">sadhana</a> is generally recited in Tibetan, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantra" title="Mantra" id="mwqA">mantra</a> portion of the practice is usually retained in its original Sanskrit.<sup about="#mwt53" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwqQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwqg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqw">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrA">]</span></span></a></sup> Whatever language is used, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Simmer-Brown" title="Judith Simmer-Brown" id="mwrQ">Judith Simmer-Brown</a> explains that a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tantra" title="Tantra" id="mwrg">tantric</a> Vajrayana text is often written in an obscure <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twilight_language" title="Twilight language" id="mwrw">twilight language</a> so that it cannot be understood by anyone without the verbal explanation of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guru" title="Guru" id="mwsA">qualified teacher</a>.<sup about="#mwt56" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwsQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsw">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwtQ">In Nepal, the use of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwtg">Sanskrit</a> as a sacred language survives in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newar_Buddhism" title="Newar Buddhism" id="mwtw">Newar Buddhism</a>, and arguably the vast majority of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_Buddhist_literature" title="Sanskrit Buddhist literature" id="mwuA">Sanskrit Buddhist manuscripts</a> have been preserved by this tradition.<sup about="#mwt60" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-14" id="mwuQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwug"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuw">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">]</span></span></a></sup> The Newar Buddhist tradition most prominently employs Sanskrit for all ritual and study purposes, and as such is the only living Buddhist Sanskrit tradition.<sup about="#mwt61" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:8_15-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":8"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:8-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:8-15" id="mwvQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvw">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwA">]</span></span></a></sup> It is distinct for its emphasis on preserving the Sanskrit originals of many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana_Sutras" title="Mahayana Sutras" class="mw-redirect" id="mwwQ">Mahayana</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vajrayana#Texts" title="Vajrayana" id="mwwg">Vajrayana scriptures</a>, which have otherwise been lost in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India" title="India" id="mwww">India</a> and survived only in translations in regions like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibet" title="Tibet" id="mwxA">Tibet</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China" title="China" id="mwxQ">China</a>. The Newar Buddhists have continued to copy <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_literature" title="Sanskrit literature" id="mwxg">Sanskrit manuscripts</a> up to the present day.<sup about="#mwt64" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_16-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:3-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-16" id="mwxw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyQ">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyg">]</span></span></a></sup> The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newar_Buddhism" title="Newar Buddhism" id="mwyw">Newar Buddhist</a> form of Vajrayana is a storehouse of ancient Sanskrit <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_texts" title="Buddhist texts" id="mwzA">Buddhist texts</a>, many of which are now only extant in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepal" title="Nepal" id="mwzQ">Nepal</a>.<sup about="#mwt67" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwzg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0A">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

<p id="mw0g"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Tamil" title="Old Tamil" id="mw0w">Old Tamil</a> was used for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_literature" title="Sangam literature" id="mw1A">Sangam</a> epics of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism" id="mw1Q">Buddhist</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism" title="Jainism" id="mw1g">Jain</a> philosophy.<sup about="#mwt71" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mw1w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2Q">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2g">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mw2w" aria-labelledby="Christianity"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Christianity">Christianity</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Christianity"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mw3A"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_(CVN_75).jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mw3Q"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_(CVN_75).jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_%28CVN_75%29.jpg/250px-US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_%28CVN_75%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_%28CVN_75%29.jpg/500px-US_Navy_040411-N-1290G-005_Navy_Chaplain_Milton_Gianulis_conducts_an_Easter_morning_Orthodox_Liturgy_candlelight_service_aboard_USS_Harry_S._Truman_%28CVN_75%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1312" data-file-type="bitmap" height="164" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mw3g"/></a><figcaption id="mw3w"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_liturgy" title="Eastern Orthodox liturgy" class="mw-redirect" id="mw4A">Eastern Orthodox liturgy</a> in the USA</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mw4Q">Christian rites, rituals, and ceremonies are not celebrated in one single sacred language. Most churches which <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_succession" title="Apostolic succession" id="mw4g">trace their origin to the Apostles</a> continue to use the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_language" title="Standard language" id="mw4w">standard languages</a> of the first few centuries AD. Many Christian churches make a distinction between a sacred language, a liturgical language, and a vernacular language. The three most important languages in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Christianity" title="Early Christianity" id="mw5A">early Christian era</a> were <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language" title="Latin language" class="mw-redirect" id="mw5Q">Latin</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language" id="mw5g">Greek</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_language" title="Syriac language" id="mw5w">Syriac</a> (a dialect of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic" id="mw6A">Aramaic</a>).<sup about="#mwt74" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mw6Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6w">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt77" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mw7Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7w">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt80" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mw8Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8w">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw9Q">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John" id="mw9g">Gospel of John</a> notes that the phrase "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus,_King_of_the_Jews" title="Jesus, King of the Jews" id="mw9w">Jesus, King of the Jews</a>" was <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titulus_Crucis" title="Titulus Crucis" id="mw-A">inscribed</a> upon the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Cross" title="True Cross" id="mw-Q">cross of Christ</a> in three different languages, thereby sanctifying them as the first languages to proclaim his divinity. These are:</p>
<ul id="mw-g"><li id="mw-w"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koine_Greek" title="Koine Greek" id="mw_A">Greek</a>, the original language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament" id="mw_Q">New Testament</a>, as well as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septuagint" title="Septuagint" id="mw_g">Septuagint</a> (a pre-Christian translation of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible" title="Hebrew Bible" id="mw_w">Hebrew Bible</a>). This was the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca" id="mwAQA">lingua franca</a> of much of the contemporary Levant.</li>
<li id="mwAQE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language" id="mwAQI">Hebrew</a>, the dominant language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Testament" title="Old Testament" id="mwAQM">Old Testament</a> (Hebrew Bible). This was the language commonly used among most Jews in the area.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt81" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"January 2026"},"reason":{"wt":"Questionable claim that Hebrew was the common language of Jerusalem in the 1st century."}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_January_2026" about="#mwt81"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt81" id="mwAQU"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="Questionable claim that Hebrew was the common language of Jerusalem in the 1st century. (January 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></li>
<li id="mwAQY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin" title="Latin" id="mwAQc">Latin</a>, the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire" id="mwAQg">Roman Empire</a>, which soon became an important language of the Christian Church, especially in the western provinces of the Roman Empire as well as being the language of the "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primus_inter_pares" title="Primus inter pares" id="mwAQk">First Among Equals</a>", the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope" title="Pope" id="mwAQo">Pope</a>, in the Christian <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentarchy" title="Pentarchy" id="mwAQs">Pentarchy</a>.</li></ul>

<p id="mwAQw">Liturgical languages are those which hold precedence within liturgy due to tradition and dispensation. Many of these languages have evolved from languages which were at one point vernacular, while some are intentional constructions by ecclesial authorities. These include:</p>
<ul id="mwAQ0"><li id="mwAQ4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Latin" title="Ecclesiastical Latin" id="mwAQ8">Ecclesiastical Latin</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_liturgical_rites" title="Latin liturgical rites" id="mwARA">Latin liturgical rites</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church" id="mwARE">Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwARI">(<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Church_Slavonic" title="Old Church Slavonic" id="mwARM">Old</a>) <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Slavonic" title="Church Slavonic" id="mwARQ">Church Slavonic</a> in several of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autocephaly" title="Autocephaly" id="mwARU">autocephalous</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Churches" title="Eastern Orthodox Churches" class="mw-redirect" id="mwARY">Eastern Orthodox Churches</a> and <i id="mwARc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sui_iuris" title="Sui iuris" id="mwARg">sui iuris</a></i> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches" id="mwARk">Eastern Catholic Churches</a></li>
<li id="mwARo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koine_Greek" title="Koine Greek" id="mwARs">Koine Greek</a> as well as the liturgical language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Church" title="Greek Orthodox Church" id="mwARw">Greek Orthodox Church</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Melkite Greek Catholic Church" id="mwAR0">Greek Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAR4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Georgian" title="Old Georgian" id="mwAR8">Old Georgian</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_Orthodox_Church" title="Georgian Orthodox Church" id="mwASA">Georgian Orthodox Church</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_Byzantine-Rite_Catholics" title="Georgian Byzantine-Rite Catholics" id="mwASE">Georgian Byzantine-Rite Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwASI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Armenian" title="Classical Armenian" id="mwASM">Classical Armenian</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church" title="Armenian Apostolic Church" id="mwASQ">Armenian Apostolic Church</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Catholic_Church" title="Armenian Catholic Church" id="mwASU">Armenian Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwASY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_language" title="Coptic language" id="mwASc">Coptic</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria" title="Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASg">Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Catholic_Church" title="Coptic Catholic Church" id="mwASk">Coptic Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwASo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geʽez" title="Geʽez" id="mwASs">Geʽez</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Orthodox_Tewahedo_Church" title="Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church" id="mwASw">Ethiopian Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eritrean_Orthodox_Tewahedo_Church" title="Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo Church" id="mwAS0">Eritrean Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Catholic_Church" title="Ethiopian Catholic Church" id="mwAS4">Ethiopian Catholic Church</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eritrean_Catholic_Church" title="Eritrean Catholic Church" id="mwAS8">Eritrean Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwATA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_language" title="Syriac language" id="mwATE">Syriac</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Christianity" title="Syriac Christianity" id="mwATI">Syriac Christianity</a> represented by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Orthodox_Church" title="Syriac Orthodox Church" id="mwATM">Syriac Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_East" title="Church of the East" id="mwATQ">Church of the East</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaldean_Catholic_Church" title="Chaldean Catholic Church" id="mwATU">Chaldean Catholic Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Catholic_Church" title="Syriac Catholic Church" id="mwATY">Syriac Catholic Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maronite_Church" title="Maronite Church" id="mwATc">Maronite Church</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christian_Churches" title="Saint Thomas Christian Churches" class="mw-redirect" id="mwATg">Saint Thomas Christian Churches</a>.</li></ul>

<p id="mwATk">The extensive use of Greek in the Roman Liturgy has continued, in theory; it was used extensively on a regular basis during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_Mass" title="Papal Mass" id="mwATo">Papal Mass</a>, which has not been celebrated for some time. By the reign of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Damasus_I" title="Pope Damasus I" id="mwATs">Pope Damasus I</a>, the continuous use of Greek in the Roman Liturgy had come to be replaced in part by Latin. Gradually, the Roman Liturgy took on more and more Latin until, generally, only a few words of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew" title="Hebrew" class="mw-redirect" id="mwATw">Hebrew</a> (e.g. <i id="mwAT0">Dominus Deus <b id="mwAT4">sabaoth</b></i>) and Greek (e.g. <i id="mwAT8"><b id="mwAUA">Kyrie eleison</b></i>) remained. The adoption of Latin was further fostered when the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vetus_Latina" title="Vetus Latina" id="mwAUE">Vetus Latina</a> (old Latin) version of the Bible was edited and parts retranslated from the original Hebrew and Greek by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Jerome" title="Saint Jerome" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAUI">Saint Jerome</a> in his <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulgate" title="Vulgate" id="mwAUM">Vulgate</a>. Latin continued as the western Church's language of liturgy and communication.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt82" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAUQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"May 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_May_2026" about="#mwt82"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt82" id="mwAUU"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAUY">In the mid-16th century the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent" id="mwAUc">Council of Trent</a> rejected a proposal to introduce <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_language" title="National language" id="mwAUg">national languages</a> as this was seen, among other reasons, as potentially divisive to Catholic unity.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt83" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAUk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"April 2011"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_April_2011" about="#mwt83"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt83" id="mwAUo"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAUs">During the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Reformation" title="English Reformation" id="mwAUw">Reformation in England</a>, when the Protestant reformers dissuaded against the use of Latin liturgy, various schools obtained a dispensation to continue to use Latin, for educational purposes. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_Uniformity_1662" title="Act of Uniformity 1662" id="mwAU0">Act of Uniformity</a> in 1662 formally prohibited use of any language for church services apart from English and Welsh, but college chapels in Oxford and Cambridge Universities were exempted and permitted to use Latin, inasmuch as the highly educated members of the universities were assumed to be familiar with the language. Though the legislation has never been overturned, Latin has been reintroduced as a liturgical language in some English churches, cathedrals and collegiate chapels influenced by the Anglo-Catholic movement.</p>

<p id="mwAU4">From the end of the 16th century, in coastal Croatia, the local vernacular language began to replace <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Slavonic" title="Church Slavonic" id="mwAU8">Church Slavonic</a> as the liturgical language. This change occurred because Church Slavonic, which had been used in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glagolitic" title="Glagolitic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVA">Glagolitic</a> liturgical books published in Rome, was becoming increasingly difficult to understand. This difficulty arose from linguistic reforms that adapted the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Slavonic_of_Croatian_recension" title="Church Slavonic of Croatian recension" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVE">Church Slavonic of Croatian recension</a> used in Croatia to the norms of Church Slavonic used in Russia. For example, the vernacular was used to ask the bride and groom if they accepted their marriage vows.</p>

<p id="mwAVI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit" title="Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVM">Jesuit</a> missionaries to China initially obtained permission to translate the Roman <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missal" title="Missal" id="mwAVQ">Missal</a> into <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese" title="Classical Chinese" id="mwAVU">Classical Chinese</a>, a scholarly form of the language. However, this permission was later revoked amid the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Rites_controversy" title="Chinese Rites controversy" id="mwAVY">Chinese Rites controversy</a>. In contrast, among the Algonquin and Iroquois peoples, missionaries were allowed to translate <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proper_(liturgy)" title="Proper (liturgy)" id="mwAVc">certain parts of the Mass</a> into their native languages.<sup about="#mwt85" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mwAVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVo">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAVw">In the 20th century, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII" id="mwAV0">Pope Pius XII</a> granted permission for a few vernaculars to be used in a few rites, rituals, and ceremonies. This did not include the Roman Liturgy of the Mass.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt86" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAV4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"May 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_May_2026" about="#mwt86"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt86" id="mwAV8"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAWA">The Catholic Church, long before the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council" id="mwAWE">Second Vatican Council</a> (Vatican II), had accepted and promoted the use of the non-vernacular liturgical languages listed above; while vernacular (i.e. modern or native) languages were also used liturgically throughout history; usually as a special concession given to religious orders conducting missionary activity.<sup about="#mwt89" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mwAWI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWQ">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAWY">In the 20th century, Vatican II set out to protect the use of Latin as a liturgical language. To a large degree, its prescription was disregarded and the vernacular not only became standard, but was generally used exclusively in the liturgy. Latin remains the chief language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_liturgical_rites" title="Latin liturgical rites" id="mwAWc">Latin liturgical rites</a> and of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_canon_law" title="Catholic canon law" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAWg">Catholic canon law</a>, but the use of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_Latin" title="Liturgical Latin" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAWk">liturgical Latin</a> is now discouraged. The use of vernacular language in liturgical practice after 1964 created controversy, and opposition to liturgical vernacular is a major tenet of the Catholic <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholic" title="Traditionalist Catholic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAWo">Traditionalist</a> movement. Meanwhile, the numerous <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches" id="mwAWs">Eastern Catholic Churches</a> in union with Rome each have their own respective parent-language.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt90" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"August 2023"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_August_2023" about="#mwt90"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt90" id="mwAW0"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAW4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox" title="Eastern Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAW8">Eastern Orthodox</a> churches vary in their use of liturgical languages. Koine Greek and Church Slavonic are the main sacred languages used in communion. Other languages are also permitted for liturgical worship, and each country often has the liturgical services in their own language. This has led to a wide variety of languages used for liturgical worship, but there is still uniformity in the liturgical worship itself.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt91" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAXA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"July 2013"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_July_2013" about="#mwt91"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt91" id="mwAXE"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAXI">Languages used in the Eastern Orthodox Church include (but are not limited to): Koine Greek, Church Slavonic, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language" id="mwAXM">Romanian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_language" title="Georgian language" id="mwAXQ">Georgian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language" title="Arabic language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAXU">Arabic</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language" title="Ukrainian language" id="mwAXY">Ukrainian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusian_language" title="Belarusian language" id="mwAXc">Belarusian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarian_language" title="Bulgarian language" id="mwAXg">Bulgarian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonian_language" title="Macedonian language" id="mwAXk">Macedonian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language" id="mwAXo">Serbian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language" id="mwAXs">Montenegrin</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language" title="English language" id="mwAXw">English</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language" title="German language" id="mwAX0">German</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language" id="mwAX4">Spanish</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language" title="French language" id="mwAX8">French</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language" title="Polish language" id="mwAYA">Polish</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language" id="mwAYE">Portuguese</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language" id="mwAYI">Italian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language" id="mwAYM">Albanian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_language" title="Finnish language" id="mwAYQ">Finnish</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_language" title="Swedish language" id="mwAYU">Swedish</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language" id="mwAYY">Chinese</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonian_language" title="Estonian language" id="mwAYc">Estonian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_language" title="Latvian language" id="mwAYg">Latvian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_language" title="Korean language" id="mwAYk">Korean</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Language" title="Japanese Language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAYo">Japanese</a>, and multiple African languages.</p>

<p id="mwAYs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox" title="Oriental Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAYw">Oriental Orthodox</a> churches outside their ancestral lands regularly pray in the local vernacular, but some clergymen and communities prefer to retain their traditional language or use a combination of languages.</p>

<p id="mwAY0">Many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anabaptism" title="Anabaptism" id="mwAY4">Anabaptist</a> groups, such as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish" title="Amish" id="mwAY8">Amish</a>, use <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_German_languages" title="High German languages" id="mwAZA">High German</a> in their worship despite not speaking it amongst themselves.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwAZE" aria-labelledby="Hinduism"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Hinduism">Hinduism</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Hinduism"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAZI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism" id="mwAZM">Hinduism</a> is traditionally considered to have <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwAZQ">Sanskrit</a> as its primary liturgical language for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_Vedic_religion" title="Historical Vedic religion" id="mwAZU">Vedic hinduism</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language" id="mwAZY">Tamil</a> as its secondary liturgical language especially for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaivism" title="Shaivism" id="mwAZc">Shaivism</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_folk_religion" title="Dravidian folk religion" id="mwAZg">Dravidian Folk Hinduism</a><sup about="#mwt94" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-24" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-24" id="mwAZk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZs">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwAZ0" aria-labelledby="Sanskrit"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Sanskrit">Sanskrit</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Sanskrit"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAZ4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwAZ8">Sanskrit</a> is the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedas" title="Vedas" id="mwAaA">Vedas</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhagavad_Gita" title="Bhagavad Gita" id="mwAaE">Bhagavad Gita</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puranas" title="Puranas" id="mwAaI">Puranas</a> like the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhagavata_Purana" title="Bhagavata Purana" id="mwAaM">Bhagavatam</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upanishads" title="Upanishads" id="mwAaQ">Upanishads</a>, the epics like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayana" title="Ramayana" id="mwAaU">Ramayana</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata" title="Mahabharata" id="mwAaY">Mahabharata</a>, and various other liturgical texts such as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahasranama" title="Sahasranama" id="mwAac">Sahasranama</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shri_Rudram_Chamakam" title="Shri Rudram Chamakam" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAag">Chamakam</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudram" title="Rudram" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAak">Rudram</a>.</p>

<p id="mwAao">Sanskrit is also the tongue of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu" title="Hindu" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAas">Hindu</a> rituals. It also has secular <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literature" title="Literature" id="mwAaw">literature</a> along with its religious canon. Most <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_theology" title="Hindu theology" id="mwAa0">Hindu theologians</a> of later centuries continued to prefer to write in Sanskrit even when it was no longer spoken as a day-to-day language. Sanskrit remains as the only liturgical link language which connects the different strains of Hinduism that are present across India. The <i id="mwAa4">de facto</i> position that Sanskrit enjoyed, as the principal language of Hinduism, enabled its survival not only in India, but also in other areas, where Hinduism thrived like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia" title="Southeast Asia" id="mwAa8">Southeast Asia</a>.<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mwAbA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbI">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwAbQ" aria-labelledby="Old_Tamil"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Old_Tamil">Old Tamil</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Old Tamil"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAbU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Tamil" title="Old Tamil" id="mwAbY">Old Tamil</a> is the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaivism" title="Shaivism" id="mwAbc">Shaiva</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tevaram" title="Tevaram" id="mwAbg">Devaram</a>) and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnavism" title="Vaishnavism" id="mwAbk">Vaishnava</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divya_Prabhandham" title="Divya Prabhandham" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAbo">Divya Prabhandham</a>) scriptures.<sup about="#mwt100" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-sangam.org_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"sangam.org"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-sangam.org-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-sangam.org-26" id="mwAbs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb0">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwAb8" aria-labelledby="Classical_Telugu"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Classical_Telugu">Classical Telugu</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Classical Telugu"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAcA">Most of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_Music" title="Carnatic Music" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAcE">Carnatic Music</a> is in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language" title="Telugu language" id="mwAcI">Telugu</a>.</p>

<p id="mwAcM">Many Hindu epics were also composed in Telugu. Some examples are the Amukthamalayada, Basava Purana, Andhra Mahabharatam, and the Ranganatha Ramayanamu.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_pages_needing_factual_verification" about="#mwt101" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAcQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"vn","href":"./Template:Vn"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"February 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_factual_verification_from_February_2026" about="#mwt101"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template " style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt101" id="mwAcU"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (February 2026)">verification needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt102" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAcY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cn","href":"./Template:Cn"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"February 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_February_2026" about="#mwt102"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt102" id="mwAcc"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAcg" aria-labelledby="Others"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Others">Others</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Others"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAck">Apart from Sanskrit, several Hindu spiritual works were composed in the various regional languages of India such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi" id="mwAco">Hindi</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assamese_language" title="Assamese language" id="mwAcs">Assamese</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Awadhi_language" title="Awadhi language" id="mwAcw">Awadhi</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhojpuri_language" title="Bhojpuri language" id="mwAc0">Bhojpuri</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language" title="Bengali language" id="mwAc4">Bengali</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odia_language" title="Odia language" id="mwAc8">Odia</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maithili_language" title="Maithili language" id="mwAdA">Maithili</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabi_language" title="Punjabi language" id="mwAdE">Punjabi</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language" title="Gujarati language" id="mwAdI">Gujarati</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada" title="Kannada" id="mwAdM">Kannada</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam" title="Malayalam" id="mwAdQ">Malayalam</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language" title="Marathi language" id="mwAdU">Marathi</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulu_language" title="Tulu language" id="mwAdY">Tulu</a>, as well as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawi_language" title="Kawi language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAdc">Old Javanese</a>,<sup about="#mwt105" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-27" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-27" id="mwAdg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdo">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAds">]</span></span></a></sup> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balinese_language" title="Balinese language" id="mwAdw">Balinese</a> of Southeast Asia.<sup about="#mwt108" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-28" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-28"}}'><a href="#cite_note-28" id="mwAd0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAd4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd8">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAeE" aria-labelledby="Islam"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Islam">Islam</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Islam"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAeI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salah" title="Salah" id="mwAeM">Salah</a>, including <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qira'at" title="Qira'at" id="mwAeQ">Quran recitation</a> must be conducted in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Arabic" title="Classical Arabic" id="mwAeU">Classical Arabic</a> (Qur'anic Arabic), which is the original language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran" title="Quran" id="mwAeY">Qur'an</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslims" title="Muslims" id="mwAec">Muslims</a> believe the Qur'an as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_revelation" title="Divine revelation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAeg">divine revelation</a>, and as such it is believed to be the direct word of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allah" title="Allah" id="mwAek">God</a>. Thus Muslims hold that the Qur'an is only truly the Qur'an if it is precisely as it was revealed—i.e., in Classical Arabic. Translations of the Qur'an into other languages are therefore not treated as the Qur'an itself; rather, they are seen as interpretive texts, which attempt to communicate a translation of the Qur'an's message.</p>

<p id="mwAeo">A number of sources require <i id="mwAes"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khutbah" title="Khutbah" id="mwAew">khutbah</a></i>, the formal Friday sermon, to be delivered completely in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Arabic" title="Classical Arabic" id="mwAe0">classical Arabic</a>.<sup about="#mwt109" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-29"}}'><a href="#cite_note-29" id="mwAe4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAe8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfA">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfE">]</span></span></a></sup> Other sources state that there is no objection if the sermon is conducted in the language understood by most attendees.<sup about="#mwt112" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-30" id="mwAfI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfQ">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAfY" aria-labelledby="Judaism"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Judaism">Judaism</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Judaism"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwAfc" aria-labelledby="Hebrew"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Hebrew">Hebrew</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Hebrew"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAfg">The core of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible" title="Hebrew Bible" id="mwAfk">Hebrew Bible</a> is written in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblical_Hebrew" title="Biblical Hebrew" id="mwAfo">Biblical Hebrew</a>, referred to by some <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews" title="Jews" id="mwAfs">Jews</a> as <i id="mwAfw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashon_Hakodesh" title="Lashon Hakodesh" id="mwAf0">Lashon Hakodesh</a></i> (<span title="Hebrew-language text" about="#mwt113" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAf4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"he"},"2":{"wt":"לשון הקודש"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="he" dir="rtl">לשון הקודש</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Hebrew-language_text" about="#mwt113" id="mwAf8"/>, "Holy Tongue"), as are most prayers in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddur" title="Siddur" id="mwAgA">Siddur</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sefer_Torah" title="Sefer Torah" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAgE">Sifrei Torah</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezuzah" title="Mezuzah" id="mwAgI">mezuzot</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tefillin" title="Tefillin" id="mwAgM">tefillin</a> are written (and heard, in the case the Sefer Torah) in Hebrew using the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_alphabet" title="Hebrew alphabet" id="mwAgQ">Assyrian script</a>.<sup about="#mwt116" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Tefillin_31-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Tefillin"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-MT-Tefillin-31"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Tefillin-31"
