id="mwAgU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgc">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgg">]</span></span></a></sup> Likewise, during Purim, the Book of Esther may be read and heard in Hebrew.<sup about="#mwt119" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Megillah_32-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Megillah"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-MT-Megillah-32"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Megillah-32" id="mwAgk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgs">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwAg0" aria-labelledby="Aramaic"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Aramaic">Aramaic</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Aramaic"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAg4">Much of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gemara" title="Gemara" id="mwAg8">Gemara</a> is written in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Aramaic_languages" title="Judeo-Aramaic languages" id="mwAhA">Aramaic</a>. Aramaic also remains the language of important liturgical and religious texts, including the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaddish" title="Kaddish" id="mwAhE">Kaddish</a> and several classical <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targum" title="Targum" id="mwAhI">translations of the Torah and the Hebrew Bibile</a>. According to halakhic sources, during Purim, the Book of Esther may be read from a translation in another language and heard in that language, including Aramaic.<sup about="#mwt120" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Megillah_32-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Megillah"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Megillah-32" id="mwAhM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhU">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhY">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwAhc" aria-labelledby="Greek"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Greek">Greek</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Greek"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAhg">According to halakhic sources, Greek is the only language other than Hebrew in which a Sifrei Torah, mezuzot, and tefillin may be written (and heard, in the case the Sefer Torah).<sup about="#mwt121" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Tefillin_31-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Tefillin"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Tefillin-31" id="mwAhk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAho"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhs">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhw">]</span></span></a></sup> The Book of Esther may likewise be read and heard in Greek.<sup about="#mwt122" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Megillah_32-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Megillah"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Megillah-32" id="mwAh0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAh4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh8">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAiE">Some halakhic authorities, however, distinguish between the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek" title="Ancient Greek" id="mwAiI">Ancient Greek</a> referred to in rabbinic sources and later forms of the language, including <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_Greek" title="Medieval Greek" id="mwAiM">Medieval Greek</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Greek" title="Modern Greek" id="mwAiQ">Modern Greek</a>; leading to discussion regarding the practical applicability of these rulings.<sup about="#mwt123" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MT-Tefillin_31-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MT-Tefillin"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MT-Tefillin-31" id="mwAiU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAic">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAig">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwAik" aria-labelledby="Ladino"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Ladino">Ladino</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Ladino"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAio">Among the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardi" title="Sephardi" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAis">Sephardim</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ladino_language" title="Ladino language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAiw">Ladino</a> was used for translations such as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrara_Bible" title="Ferrara Bible" id="mwAi0">Ferrara Bible</a>. It was also used during the Sephardi liturgy. Ladino is also often referred to as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Spanish" title="Judeo-Spanish" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAi4">Judeo-Spanish</a>, as it is a dialect of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castilian_language" title="Castilian language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAi8">Castilian</a> used by Sephardim as an everyday language until the 20th century.<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Sephiha_33-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Sephiha"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Sephiha-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Sephiha-33" id="mwAjA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjI">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt126" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Cohen_34-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Cohen"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Cohen-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Cohen-34" id="mwAjQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjY">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwAjg" aria-labelledby="List"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="List">List</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: List"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt127" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAjk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"More citations needed section","href":"./Template:More_citations_needed_section"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"December 2011"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1383368056" about="#mwt128" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Message box/ambox.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><table class="box-More_citations_needed_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation" about="#mwt127"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail <ul id="mwAjo"><li id="mwAjs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkadian_language" title="Akkadian language" id="mwAjw">Akkadian</a> was a long used liturgical language.</li>
<li id="mwAj0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic" id="mwAj4">Aramaic</a>, used in some later books of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanakh" title="Tanakh" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAj8">Tanakh</a>, some Jewish prayers, and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talmud" title="Talmud" id="mwAkA">Talmud</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAkE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avestan" title="Avestan" id="mwAkI">Avestan</a>, the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avesta" title="Avesta" id="mwAkM">Avesta</a>, the sacred texts of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism" title="Zoroastrianism" id="mwAkQ">Zoroastrianism</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAkU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahasa_tanah" title="Bahasa tanah" id="mwAkY">Bahasa tanah</a>, a sacred language used in special traditional ceremonies of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfur_people" title="Alfur people" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAkc">Alfur people</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maluku_Islands" title="Maluku Islands" id="mwAkg">Maluku</a> and is generally written using the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alifuru_script" title="Alifuru script" id="mwAkk">Alifuru script</a>. It literally means 'language of the land'.</li>
<li id="mwAko"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balaibalan" title="Balaibalan" id="mwAks">Balaibalan</a> was invented in the context of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi" title="Sufi" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAkw">Sufi</a> devotion, although it was only briefly used.</li>
<li id="mwAk0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Bengali" title="Christian Bengali" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAk4">Christian Bengali</a>, the language of Christian worship and Bengali Christian literature restricted to the Anglo-Bengali Christian community</li>
<li id="mwAk8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Arabic" title="Classical Arabic" id="mwAlA">Classical Arabic</a>, the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran" title="Quran" id="mwAlE">Quran</a>; it differs from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varieties_of_Arabic" title="Varieties of Arabic" id="mwAlI">various forms of contemporary spoken Arabic</a> in lexical and grammatical areas.</li>
<li id="mwAlM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese" title="Classical Chinese" id="mwAlQ">Classical Chinese</a>, the language of older Chinese literature and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism" title="Confucianism" id="mwAlU">Confucian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism" title="Taoism" id="mwAlY">Taoist</a>, and in East Asia also of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana_Buddhism" title="Mahayana Buddhism" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAlc">Mahayana Buddhist</a> sacred texts. The current pronunciation of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters" title="Chinese characters" id="mwAlg">Chinese characters</a> is based on local pronunciation, for example <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Japan" title="Buddhism in Japan" id="mwAlk">Japanese Buddhists</a> read <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_texts" title="Buddhist texts" id="mwAlo">Buddhist texts</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_language" title="Japanese language" id="mwAls">Japanese</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanji" title="Kanji" id="mwAlw">Kanji</a> pronunciation.</li>
<li id="mwAl0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_language" title="Coptic language" id="mwAl4">Coptic</a>, a form of ancient <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_language" title="Egyptian language" id="mwAl8">Egyptian</a>, is used by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria" title="Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAmA">Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Catholic_Church" title="Coptic Catholic Church" id="mwAmE">Coptic Catholic Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAmI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Czech_language#Old_Czech" title="History of the Czech language" id="mwAmM">Old Czech</a> is used by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moravian_Church" title="Moravian Church" id="mwAmQ">Moravian Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAmU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damin" title="Damin" id="mwAmY">Damin</a>, an initiation language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lardil_people" title="Lardil people" id="mwAmc">Lardil people</a> in Australia.</li>
<li id="mwAmg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language" id="mwAmk">Early Modern Dutch</a> is the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statenvertaling" title="Statenvertaling" id="mwAmo">Statenvertaling</a>, still in use among orthodox Calvinist denominations in the Netherlands.</li>
<li id="mwAms"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Modern_English" title="Early Modern English" id="mwAmw">Early Modern English</a> is used in some parts of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion" id="mwAm0">Anglican Communion</a> and by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_Anglican_movement" title="Continuing Anglican movement" id="mwAm4">Continuing Anglican movement</a>, as well as by a variety of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_James_Only_movement" title="King James Only movement" id="mwAm8">English-speaking Protestants</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAnA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskayan" title="Eskayan" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAnE">Eskayan</a> in the Philippines.</li>
<li id="mwAnI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscan_language" title="Etruscan language" id="mwAnM">Etruscan</a>, cultivated for religious and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magic_and_religion" title="Magic and religion" id="mwAnQ">magical</a> purposes in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire" id="mwAnU">Roman Empire</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAnY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geʽez" title="Geʽez" id="mwAnc">Geʽez</a>, the predecessor of many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Semitic_languages" title="Ethiopian Semitic languages" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAng">Ethiopian Semitic languages</a> (e.g. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amharic" title="Amharic" id="mwAnk">Amharic</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigrinya_language" title="Tigrinya language" id="mwAno">Tigrinya</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigre_language" title="Tigre language" id="mwAns">Tigre</a>) used as a liturgical language by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beta_Israel" title="Beta Israel" id="mwAnw">Ethiopian Jews</a> and by Ethiopian and Eritrean <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity" id="mwAn0">Christians</a> (in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Orthodox_Tewahedo_Church" title="Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church" id="mwAn4">Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eritrean_Orthodox_Tewahedo_Church" title="Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo Church" id="mwAn8">Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo Church</a>, and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" title="Roman Catholic Church" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAoA">Roman Catholic Church</a>).</li>
<li id="mwAoE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_New_High_German" title="Early New High German" id="mwAoI">Early New High German</a> is used in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish" title="Amish" id="mwAoM">Amish</a> communities for Bible readings and sermons.</li>
<li id="mwAoQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_language" title="Gothic language" id="mwAoU">Gothic</a>, the sole East Germanic language which is attested by significant texts, is usually considered to have been preserved by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arianism" title="Arianism" id="mwAoY">Arian churches</a>, while the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goths" title="Goths" id="mwAoc">Goths</a> themselves spoke <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulgar_Latin" title="Vulgar Latin" id="mwAog">vulgar Latin</a> dialects of their areas.</li>
<li id="mwAok"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koine_Greek" title="Koine Greek" id="mwAoo">Koine Greek</a>, the language of early <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauline_Christianity" title="Pauline Christianity" id="mwAos">Pauline Christianity</a> and all of its <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament" id="mwAow">New Testament</a> books. It is today the liturgical language of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Christianity" title="Greek Orthodox Christianity" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAo0">Greek Orthodox Christianity</a> and several other directly Greek connected <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Churches" title="Eastern Orthodox Churches" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAo4">Eastern Orthodox Churches</a>. It differs markedly from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Greek" title="Modern Greek" id="mwAo8">Modern Greek</a> but remains comprehensible for Modern Greek speakers.</li>
<li id="mwApA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habla_Congo" title="Habla Congo" id="mwApE">Habla Congo</a> (or <i id="mwApI">Habla Bantu</i>) is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kongo_language" title="Kongo language" id="mwApM">Kongo</a>-based <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_language" title="Liturgical language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwApQ">liturgical language</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_religion" title="Palo religion" class="mw-redirect" id="mwApU">Palo religion</a> with origins in Cuba, later spreading to other countries in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Basin" title="Caribbean Basin" id="mwApY">Caribbean Basin</a>.</li>
<li id="mwApc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hattic_language" title="Hattic language" id="mwApg">Hattic</a> was used by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hittites" title="Hittites" id="mwApk">Hittites</a> who spoke an unrelated language.</li>
<li id="mwApo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblical_Hebrew" title="Biblical Hebrew" id="mwAps">Biblical Hebrew</a> – the languages in which the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible" title="Hebrew Bible" id="mwApw">Hebrew Bible</a> has been written over time; these differ from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Hebrew" title="Modern Hebrew" id="mwAp0">today's spoken Hebrew</a> in lexical and grammatical areas.</li>
<li id="mwAp4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_Maroon_Spirit_Possession_Language" title="Jamaican Maroon Spirit Possession Language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAp8">Jamaican Maroon Spirit Possession Language</a>, spoken by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_Maroons" title="Jamaican Maroons" id="mwAqA">Jamaican Maroons</a>, the descendants of runaway slaves in the mountains of Jamaica, during their "Kromanti Play", a ceremony in which the participants are said to be <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spirit_possession" title="Spirit possession" id="mwAqE">possessed by their ancestors</a> and to speak as their ancestors did centuries ago.</li>
<li id="mwAqI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Middle_Japanese" title="Early Middle Japanese" id="mwAqM">Early Middle Japanese</a> is chanted in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinto" title="Shinto" id="mwAqQ">Shinto</a> rituals.</li>
<li id="mwAqU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Javanese" title="Old Javanese" id="mwAqY">Old Javanese</a> is utilized in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kejawèn" title="Kejawèn" id="mwAqc">Kejawèn</a>, the polytheistic <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_religion" title="Ethnic religion" id="mwAqg">ethnic religion</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_people" title="Javanese people" id="mwAqk">Javanese people</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAqo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kallawaya_language" title="Kallawaya language" id="mwAqs">Kallawaya</a>, a secret medicinal language used in the Andes.</li>
<li id="mwAqw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Latin" title="Ecclesiastical Latin" id="mwAq0">Ecclesiastical Latin</a> is the liturgical language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church" title="Latin Church" id="mwAq4">Latin Church</a>'s <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_liturgical_rites" title="Latin liturgical rites" id="mwAq8">Latin liturgical rites</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church" id="mwArA">Catholic Church</a>. It is based on the Italian pronunciation.</li>
<li id="mwArE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Latin" title="Old Latin" id="mwArI">Old Latin</a> was used in various prayers in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Roman_religion" title="Ancient Roman religion" class="mw-redirect" id="mwArM">Roman paganism</a>, such as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_Arvale" title="Carmen Arvale" id="mwArQ">Carmen Arvale</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_Saliare" title="Carmen Saliare" id="mwArU">Carmen Saliare</a>. These texts were unintelligible to classical Latin speakers and remain somewhat obscure to scholars even today.</li>
<li id="mwArY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchu_language" title="Manchu language" id="mwArc">Manchu</a> was the language used in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamanism_in_the_Qing_dynasty#The_.22Shamanic_Code.22_of_1747" title="Shamanism in the Qing dynasty" class="mw-redirect" id="mwArg">Manchu shamanic rituals</a>.</li>
<li id="mwArk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandaic_language" title="Mandaic language" id="mwAro">Mandaic</a>, an Aramaic language, in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandaeanism" title="Mandaeanism" class="mw-redirect" id="mwArs">Mandaeanism</a>.</li>
<li id="mwArw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://simple.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical%20Meitei" title="simple:Classical Meitei" class="extiw" id="mwAr0">Classical Meitei</a>, the holy language of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanamahism" title="Sanamahism" id="mwAr4">Sanamahism</a> (Meitei religion).</li>
<li id="mwAr8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Mongolian_language" title="Classical Mongolian language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAsA">Classical Mongolian</a> was used alongside Classical Tibetan as sacred languages of Tibetan Buddhism in Mongolia.</li>
<li id="mwAsE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Norse" title="Old Norse" id="mwAsI">Old Norse</a>, used in some <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heathenry_(new_religious_movement)" title="Heathenry (new religious movement)" id="mwAsM">Heathenry</a> groups as a religious language</li>
<li id="mwAsQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaic_language" title="Palaic language" id="mwAsU">Palaic</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luwian_language" title="Luwian language" id="mwAsY">Luwian</a>, cultivated as a religious language by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hittites" title="Hittites" id="mwAsc">Hittites</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAsg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali" title="Pali" id="mwAsk">Pali</a>, the original language of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada" title="Theravada" id="mwAso">Theravada</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism" id="mwAss">Buddhism</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAsw">Some <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language" id="mwAs0">Portuguese</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin" title="Latin" id="mwAs4">Latin</a> prayers are retained by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakure_Kirishitan" title="Kakure Kirishitan" id="mwAs8">Kakure Kirishitan</a> (Hidden Christians) of Japan, who recite it without understanding the language.</li>
<li id="mwAtA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sant_Bhasha" title="Sant Bhasha" id="mwAtE">Sant Bhasha</a>, a mélange of archaic <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabi_language" title="Punjabi language" id="mwAtI">Punjabi</a> and several other languages, is the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikh" title="Sikh" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAtM">Sikh</a> holy scripture <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guru_Granth_Sahib" title="Guru Granth Sahib" id="mwAtQ">Guru Granth Sahib</a>.<sup about="#mwt131" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Dass2000_35-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Dass2000"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Dass2000-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Dass2000-35" id="mwAtU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtc">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtg">]</span></span></a></sup> It is different from the various dialects of Punjabi that exist today.</li>
<li id="mwAtk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedic" title="Vedic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAto">Vedic</a> &amp; Classical <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit" id="mwAts">Sanskrit</a>, the dialects of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veda" title="Veda" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAtw">Vedas</a> and other sacred texts of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism" id="mwAt0">Hinduism</a> as well as the original language of several sects of early <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism" id="mwAt4">Buddhism</a> and a language of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism" title="Jainism" id="mwAt8">Jainism</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAuA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Church_Slavonic" title="Old Church Slavonic" id="mwAuE">Old Church Slavonic</a>, also called <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Bulgarian" title="Old Bulgarian" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAuI">Old Bulgarian</a>, the liturgical language of the Slavic Eastern Orthodoxy</li>
<li id="mwAuM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Slavonic_language" title="Church Slavonic language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAuQ">Church Slavonic</a> is the current liturgical language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church" id="mwAuU">Russian Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church" id="mwAuY">Serbian Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarian_Orthodox_Church" title="Bulgarian Orthodox Church" id="mwAuc">Bulgarian Orthodox Church</a>  and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonian_Orthodox_Church" title="Macedonian Orthodox Church" id="mwAug">Macedonian Orthodox Church</a> and certain <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine" title="Byzantine" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAuk">Byzantine</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruthenian_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church" id="mwAuo">Ruthenian</a>) <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Catholic" title="Eastern Catholic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAus">Eastern Catholic</a> churches.</li>
<li id="mwAuw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumerian_language" title="Sumerian language" id="mwAu0">Sumerian</a>, cultivated and preserved in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyria" title="Assyria" id="mwAu4">Assyria</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylon" title="Babylon" id="mwAu8">Babylon</a> long after its extinction as an everyday language.</li>
<li id="mwAvA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_language" title="Syriac language" id="mwAvE">Syriac</a>, a dialect of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic" id="mwAvI">Aramaic</a>, is used as a liturgical language by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Christianity" title="Syriac Christianity" id="mwAvM">Syriac Christians</a> who belong to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaldean_Catholic_Church" title="Chaldean Catholic Church" id="mwAvQ">Chaldean Catholic Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_Church_of_the_East" title="Assyrian Church of the East" id="mwAvU">Assyrian Church of the East</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Orthodox_Church" title="Syriac Orthodox Church" id="mwAvY">Syriac Orthodox Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Catholic_Church" title="Syriac Catholic Church" id="mwAvc">Syriac Catholic Church</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syro_Malabar_Catholic_Church" title="Syro Malabar Catholic Church" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAvg">Syro Malabar Catholic Church</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maronite_Church" title="Maronite Church" id="mwAvk">Maronite Church</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAvo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Tamil" title="Old Tamil" id="mwAvs">Old Tamil</a> is the language of the Shaiva (Devaram) and Vaishnava (Divya Prabhandham) scriptures.<sup about="#mwt132" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-sangam.org_26-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"sangam.org"}}'><a href="#cite_note-sangam.org-26" id="mwAvw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAv0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv4">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv8">]</span></span></a></sup></li>
<li id="mwAwA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Tibetan" title="Classical Tibetan" id="mwAwE">Classical Tibetan</a>, known as <i id="mwAwI">Chhokey</i> in Bhutan, the sacred language of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism" title="Tibetan Buddhism" id="mwAwM">Tibetan Buddhism</a>.</li>
<li id="mwAwQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language" title="Yoruba language" id="mwAwU">Yoruba</a> (known as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucumi_language" title="Lucumi language" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAwY">Lucumi</a> in Cuba), the language of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people" title="Yoruba people" id="mwAwc">Yoruba people</a>, brought to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_World" title="New World" id="mwAwg">New World</a> by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-atlantic_slave_trade" title="Trans-atlantic slave trade" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAwk">enslaved Africans</a>, and preserved in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santería" title="Santería" id="mwAwo">Santería</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candomblé" title="Candomblé" id="mwAws">Candomblé</a>, and other African diasporic religions. The Yoruba descendants in these communities, as well as non-descendants that have adopted one of the Yoruba-based religions in the diaspora, no longer speak any of the Yoruba dialects with any level of fluency, and the liturgical usage also reflects the compromise of the language whereby there does not exist an understanding of correct grammar nor proper intonation.  Spirit possession by the Yoruba deities in Cuba shows that the deity manifested in the devotee at a Cuban orisa ceremony delivers messages to the faithful in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bozal" title="Bozal" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAww">Bozal</a>, a type of Spanish-based creole with some words of Yoruba language as well as those of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_languages" title="Bantu languages" id="mwAw0">Bantu</a> origin with an inflection similar to the way Africans would speak as they were learning Spanish during enslavement.</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwAw4" aria-labelledby="References"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="References">References</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sacred_language&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: References"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<li about="#cite_note-Dass2000-35" id="cite_note-Dass2000-35" data-mw-footnote-number="35"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwBHs"><a href="#cite_ref-Dass2000_35-0" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwBHw"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwBH0">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-Dass2000-35" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt129" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwBH4" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cite book ","href":"./Template:Cite_book"},"params":{"author":{"wt":"Nirmal Dass"},"title":{"wt":"Songs of Saints from Adi Granth"},"url":{"wt":"https://books.google.com/books?id=GZSndbFnrVIC&amp;pg=PA13"},"access-date":{"wt":"29 November 2012"},"year":{"wt":"2000"},"publisher":{"wt":"SUNY Press"},"isbn":{"wt":"978-0-7914-4684-3"},"page":{"wt":"13"},"quote":{"wt":"Any attempt at translating songs from the Adi Granth certainly involves working not with one language, but several, along with dialectical differences. The languages used by the saints range from Sanskrit; regional Prakrits; western, eastern and southern Apabhramsa; and Sahaskrit.  More particularly, we find sant bhasha, Marathi, Old Hindi, central and Lehndi Panjabi, Sindhi and Persian. There are also many dialects deployed, such as Purbi Marwari, Bangru, Dakhni, Malwai, and Awadhi."}},"i":0}}]}'/><cite id="CITEREFNirmal_Dass2000" class="citation book cs1" about="#mwt129">Nirmal Dass (2000). <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=GZSndbFnrVIC&amp;pg=PA13" class="external text" id="mwBH8"><i id="mwBIA">Songs of Saints from Adi Granth</i></a>. SUNY Press. p.<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwBIE"> </span>13. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" title="ISBN (identifier)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBII">ISBN</a><span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwBIM"> </span><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-7914-4684-3" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-7914-4684-3" id="mwBIQ"><bdi id="mwBIU">978-0-7914-4684-3</bdi></a><span class="reference-accessdate" id="mwBIY">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwBIc">29 November</span> 2012</span>. <q id="mwBIg">Any attempt at translating songs from the Adi Granth certainly involves working not with one language, but several, along with dialectical differences. The languages used by the saints range from Sanskrit; regional Prakrits; western, eastern and southern Apabhramsa; and Sahaskrit.  More particularly, we find sant bhasha, Marathi, Old Hindi, central and Lehndi Panjabi, Sindhi and Persian. There are also many dialects deployed, such as Purbi Marwari, Bangru, Dakhni, Malwai, and Awadhi.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Songs+of+Saints+from+Adi+Granth&amp;rft.pages=13&amp;rft.pub=SUNY+Press&amp;rft.date=2000&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-7914-4684-3&amp;rft.au=Nirmal+Dass&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DGZSndbFnrVIC%26pg%3DPA13&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASacred+language" class="Z3988" about="#mwt129" id="mwBIk"></span></span></li>
</ol></div></div>
